**Journal.** `AV created {Tag} usable={t} value={v}`; `AV expired ({why}) {Tag} at {t}`; `AV invalidated (roll) {Tag} at {t}`; `AV p2Degraded …`.

**Tests.** `tests/AvwapTests`.

**Snippet.** Anchors VWAPs at confirmed swings and gap opens, then each bar looks for a long pullback: the bar's low reaches the −1σ band of an up-anchored VWAP (one started at a swing low or a gap up). `usable` false means the anchor is expired, has no value yet, or is not yet confirmed; skip it.

```csharp
// Create
protected override void Create()
{
	base.Create();
	AvwapEvents = EAvwapAnchor.Swing             // anchor at confirmed swing highs and lows (forces PRICEACTION on)
		| EAvwapAnchor.GapOpen;                  // and at RTH gap opens
	AvwapLimits.maxAgeSessions = 5;              // drop anchors older than 5 completed sessions
}

// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();                              // the manager steps here, after PRICEACTION
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	_AvwapManager am = AvwapManager;
	if (am == null) return;                      // AvwapEvents == None
	foreach (_Avwap a in am.Anchors)             // live anchors, oldest first
	{
		bool usable = a.Valid                        // not expired, value defined
			&& a.UsableFrom <= Bars.Time[0];         // no lookahead: swing anchors only from pivot confirmation
		bool upAnchor = a.Direction > 0;             // swing low or gap up
		bool atMinus1Sigma = Bars.Low[0] <= a.Band(-1);   // Band(-1) = Value - 1 x Sigma
		if (usable && upAnchor && atMinus1Sigma)
		{
			/* long pullback to the -1 sigma band of an up-anchored VWAP */
		}
	}
}
```

### 4.8 PRICEACTION `_PriceActionEngine`

**Why it exists.** Market structure (swing highs and lows, higher-highs / lower-lows, breaks of structure) is the vocabulary of discretionary traders but is easy to code with lookahead, because a swing is only known some bars after its extreme. The engine finds pivots on several layers (minor and major swings), labels them, detects breaks of structure (BOS) and changes of character (CHoCH), and flags candle and volume patterns, each only from the bar on which it is confirmed.

**When to use it.** Structural stops (below the last higher-low), trend filters from the higher layer, breakout triggers: examples 19, 26, 28, 31 and 34 (31 and 34 also run extra engines on other streams).

**Enable.** `PriceActionConfig.enabled = true` in `Create()`. Extra instances: `ExtraPriceAction.Add(new PriceActionConfig { enabled = true, dataStream = 2, role = "htf" })`.

**`PriceActionConfig` defaults.**
- Stream and pivots: `dataStream 1`, `pivotMode Fractal`, `tieRule Strict`, `layers 2`, `strength 1`.
- ATR and volume: `atrLength 20`, `volumeLength 20`, `volumeIncludesCurrent true`, `atrMultiple 1.5`.
- Candle factors: `factorDoji 0.1`, `factorClose 0.1`, `factorStrong 1.0`, `factorEpsilon 0.05`, `equalFactor 0.1`.
- Breakouts and history: `lookbackPivots 5`, `minBreakoutLayer 1` (main engine; the DVP-hosted engines use 0, §5.31), `breakoutNeedsStrong true`, `history 50`, `candles true`.
- Presets: `preset Library`, `role ""`; `ApplyPreset(EPriceActionPreset)`, `ApplyPresetDefaults`. The Futures preset uses `LeftInclusive` and `volumeIncludesCurrent = false`; the futures bases apply it unless you called `ApplyPreset` yourself.

**API.**

| Member | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| `IsWarm` | Enough bars for ATR and volume statistics. |
| `Atr`, `VolumeMean`, `VolumeSigma` | The engine's own ATR and volume statistics. |
| `Candle` (`ECandle` flags), `Has(ECandle)` | Candle pattern flags of the last closed bar (doji, strong close, breakout candle …). |
| `Changed` | Candle flags that differ from the previous bar's flags. |
| `LastBarTime`, `LastBar` | Which bar was last processed. |
| `NewPivotHigh`, `NewPivotLow` | A layer-0 pivot was confirmed on this bar. |
| `BosUp`, `BosDown`, `ChochUp`, `ChochDown` | This bar closed beyond the last swing high / low (break of structure); CHoCH = a break against the structure before the break. |
| `LastHigh(layer = 0)`, `LastLow(layer = 0)`, `High(layer, ago)`, `Low(layer, ago)`, `PivotCount(layer, isHigh)` | Confirmed pivots, newest first. |
| `Structure(layer = 0)` → `EStructure {Unknown, Up, Down, Range}` | Trend state of a layer. |
| `DoubleTop(layer)`, `DoubleBottom(layer)` | The last pivot high (low) is equal, within `equalFactor`, to an earlier one (`isEqual`). |
| `_Pivot` fields `isHigh`, `layer`, `seq`, `price`, `extremeTime`, `extremeBar`, `confirmTime`, `confirmBar`, `label` (`EPivotLabel` HH/LH/HL/LL), `isEqual`, `equalToSeq`, `legSizeAtr` | Where the extreme was, when it was confirmed, and how it relates to the previous pivot. |
| `PriceActionFor(int dataStream, string role = null)` | Find an extra engine by stream and role. |

**Timing.** Steps on its own stream's closed bars. A pivot exists only from `confirmTime` (after `strength` bars) and is never relabelled. The engine resets on a roll.

**Journal.** `PA{ds} L{layer} PivotHigh|PivotLow #{seq} {price} {label} [EQ#n] ext={t} conf={t}`; `… BreakoutUp|BreakoutDown L{layer} of #{seq} {price} at {t}`; `… BOS up|down [CHoCH] at {t}`; `… roll reset at {t}`; `PA{ds}-{role} created strength() layers() shared()`.

**Tests.** `tests/PriceActionTests` (`McShim.cs`).

**Snippet.** A bullish break of structure in an up-trending higher layer, with a structural stop 2 ticks under the last confirmed swing low. `stop = NaN` means no swing low exists yet; do not trade.

```csharp
// Create
protected override void Create()
{
	base.Create();
	PriceActionConfig.ApplyPreset(EPriceActionPreset.Futures);   // the futures bases do this for you
	PriceActionConfig.enabled = true;
}

// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();                              // the engine steps on its stream's closed bar here
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	_PriceActionEngine pa = PriceActionEngine;
	if (pa == null || !pa.IsWarm) return;
	bool bullishBreak = pa.BosUp                 // this bar broke the last pivot high
		&& pa.Structure(1) == EStructure.Up;     // and the higher layer is in an up-trend
	if (!bullishBreak) return;
	_Pivot hl = pa.LastLow(0);                   // last confirmed layer-0 pivot low
	double stop = hl != null ? hl.price - 2 * tickSize() : double.NaN;   // structural stop 2 ticks under it
}
```

### 4.9 M-TOD `_TodNormaliser`

**Why it exists.** Volume and range follow a strong daily pattern: the first minutes after 09:30 ET are always busy, lunch is always quiet. "Volume is high" only means something compared with the same time on other days. M-TOD keeps, for each minute-of-session bucket, the values of prior completed sessions and reports how unusual this bar is for its time of day.

**When to use it.** Volume-climax or quiet-market filters, and "is this opening range wide for an opening range?": examples 4 (volume pool), 19 (range median) and 25 (true range and volume z).

**Enable.** `TodMetrics = ETodMetric.Volume | ETodMetric.Range …` in `Create()` (`ETodMetric {None, Volume, Delta, Range, TrueRange}`).

**Defaults.** `TodSessions 20` (sessions in the norm), `TodMinSessions 10` (needed before `Valid`), `TodBucketMinutes 0`. The host resolves the bucket in `_CustomSignal` (line 882): 0 → the Data1 bar size when Data1 is a minute chart, else 1 minute.

**API.**

| Member | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| `Valid(m)` | Enough prior sessions in this bar's bucket. |
| `Z(m)` | z-score of this bar vs the same bucket in prior sessions (3 = three σ above normal for this time). |
| `Median(m)`, `Pct(m)` | The bucket's median; the fraction (0–1) of prior sessions at or below this bar. |
| `ZCum(m)` | Session-to-date cumulative value vs the cumulative norm (e.g. "volume so far today is unusually high"). |
| `Sessions(m)` | Sessions currently in the norm. |
| `VolumePool(minuteOfSession)` | The raw prior-session volumes of a bucket (copy, oldest first). |

**Timing.** Norms come from prior completed sessions. Half-days are excluded (via `todHalfDay`). Range and TR are dropped on the roll-splice bar. Delta requires the futures base order flow.

**Journal.** `TOD delta tier change a -> b: norm reset at session {day}`.

**Tests.** `tests/TodTests`.

**Snippet.** Flags a volume climax: this bar's volume is more than 3 σ above the normal volume for this minute of the session. `climax` is false during warm-up (fewer than `TodMinSessions` sessions).

```csharp
// Create
protected override void Create()
{
	base.Create();
	TodMetrics = ETodMetric.Volume;              // norm volume per minute-of-session bucket
}

// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	_TodNormaliser tod = TodNormaliser;
	bool climax = tod != null
		&& tod.Valid(ETodMetric.Volume)          // enough prior sessions for this bucket
		&& tod.Z(ETodMetric.Volume) > 3;         // volume > 3 sigma above normal for this time of day
}
```

### 4.10 M-XMKT `_XmktLeg`

**Why it exists.** Related markets lead or confirm each other (NQ vs ES, ES vs bonds). M-XMKT reads another market from an extra chart stream, aligns it to Data1 on closed bars only, and gives you returns, rolling correlation, beta and a relative-strength z-score. Orders stay on Data1.

**When to use it.** Relative-strength or divergence filters: example 39.

**Enable.** `XmktLegs.Add(new XmktLegConfig { role = "NQ", dataStream = 2 })` in `Create()`.

**Defaults.** `role "leg"`, `dataStream 2`, `maxStaleSec 0` (= 2× the leg bar; off for non-time bars), `returnBars 1`, `corrLength 60`, `rsLength 60`, `leaderSwing false`.

**API.**

| Member | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| `XmktLeg(role)` → `_XmktLeg` | The leg, or null when not configured. |
| `Available`, `LastLegTime`, `LastClose` | The leg has a fresh closed bar (not stale); its time and close. |
| `LogReturn`, `Data1LogReturn` | Log returns of the leg and of Data1 over `returnBars`. |
| `Correlation`, `Beta` | Rolling correlation and beta of the leg vs Data1 (`corrLength` bars). |
| `RsZ` | z-score of ln(leg / Data1) vs its rolling window: negative = the leg is weak relative to Data1. |
| `XmktLeaderSwing(role)` | A PRICEACTION engine on the leg (with `leaderSwing = true`), or null. |

**Timing.** The leg is fed its last closed bar with `Time <= Data1 time` (`htfMapped`). It becomes unavailable when stale. Leg rolls are not inherited from Data1.

**Journal.** `XMKT {role} Data{n} available at {t} (staleness limit …)`; Info `XMKT leg … symbol() resolution() dataFeed() maxStale()`.

**Tests.** `tests/XmktTests`.

**Snippet.** NQ on Data2 as a leg; the strategy reacts when NQ is unusually weak against ES. `RsZ` is NaN until `rsLength` bars are in the window.

```csharp
// Create
protected override void Create()
{
	base.Create();
	XmktLegs.Add(new XmktLegConfig { role = "NQ", dataStream = 2 });   // NQ on Data2; orders stay on Data1
}

// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	_XmktLeg nq = XmktLeg("NQ");
	if (nq == null || !nq.Available) return;     // no closed leg bar yet, or the leg is stale
	if (nq.RsZ < -2)
	{
		/* ln(NQ / Data1) is 2 sigma below its rolling mean: NQ unusually weak vs Data1 */
	}
}
```

### 4.11 M-TICK `_TickStream`

**Why it exists.** A minute bar hides how it traded: 2,000 contracts in one burst of prints reads differently from the same volume spread evenly. M-TICK loads the 1-tick Trade data behind each Data1 bar (through a DataLoader request, so the chart itself stays on minute bars) and summarises it per bar: print count, peak prints per second, gaps between prints, and a burst score.

**When to use it.** Filters on print intensity ("only take the breakout if the tape is bursting"): example 37.

**Enable.** `TickConfig.enabled = true` in `Create()`. Nothing is requested while it is off.

**Defaults.** `historyTicks 2,000,000`, `realTime true`, `burstZ 3`, `burstNormLength 200`, `burstMinSamples 30`, `sizeBuckets {1,2,5,10,20,50}`, `largePrintSize 20`, `sizeOutputsVerified false`.

**API.** `TickStats` returns the last closed bar's `TickBarStats`, or null.

| Field | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| `valid`, `quality` | The tick data for this bar arrived complete and on time (`quality` "Tick"); otherwise do not use the other fields. |
| `prints`, `maxPrintsPerSecond` | Number of trades in the bar; the busiest second. |
| `interPrintMedianMs`, `interPrintP10Ms` | Typical and short (10th percentile) gaps between trades. |
| `burstZ`, `burst` | z-score of this bar's busiest-second print count vs the previous `burstNormLength` bars (NaN until `burstMinSamples` bars); `burst` = z ≥ `TickConfig.burstZ`. |
| `sizeHistogram`, `largePrints`, `volume` | Trade-size counts and large prints; null / −1 / NaN until `sizeOutputsVerified` is set. |

**Timing.** Aggregated over `(Time[1], Time[0]]` at bar close. Late ticks are dropped.

**Journal.** `TICK DataLoader 1-tick Trade request for {sym}, N ticks back, RT=…`; `TICK n late tick(s) dropped …`; `TICK data missing/late … outputs invalid`; `TICK data valid again …`.

**VERIFY.** The `Bar` member names are read by reflection (`Time`, `Close`, `TotalVolume`/`Volume`/`Ticks`); the code flags this as "verify in the editor".

**Tests.** `tests/TickTests`.

**Snippet.** Turns the tick request on and reads the burst flag of the bar that just closed. `ts == null` or `!ts.valid` means the tick data did not cover this bar; the strategy treats that as "no information", not as "no burst".

```csharp
// Create
protected override void Create()
{
	base.Create();
	TickConfig.enabled = true;                   // starts a DataLoader 1-tick Trade request
	TickConfig.burstZ = 3.0;                     // burst = prints/s z-score >= 3
}

// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	TickBarStats ts = TickStats;                 // stats of the bar that just closed, or null
	if (ts == null || !ts.valid) return;         // tick data missing or late: do not trade on it
	bool burst = ts.burst;                       // a print-rate burst inside this bar
}
```

### 4.12 M-MTF (`_MtfMath`, host helpers)

**Why it exists.** Reading a higher-timeframe stream (Data2 = 60 m) from a 5 m chart is the classic source of lookahead: the 60 m bar is still forming, so its high is not final. M-MTF gives indices and values of the last **closed** higher-timeframe bar only, and checks at start that the stream is the same instrument.

**When to use it.** Higher-timeframe levels and trend filters on the same instrument: examples 2, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11, 13 and 14. For a different market use M-XMKT (§4.10).

**Enable.** `HtfStreams = new[] { 2 }` before `base.StartCalc()`. StartCalc throws if the stream is missing or carries another root symbol.

**API.**

| Member | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| `HtfClosedIndex(ds, ago = 0)` | Bars-ago index of the ago-th closed bar of stream `ds` (0 if bar 0 has closed, else 1). |
| `HtfClosed(ds, ago = 0)` | Close of that bar. |
| `HtfClosedBar(ds, ago, out time, out o, out h, out l, out c, out v)` | All fields of that bar. |
| `htfMapped(ds, maxBack = 2)` | Index of the last closed bar of `ds` whose time is not after Data1's; −1 = none. |
| `HtfForming(ds)` | Deliberate access to the still-forming bar (values change intrabar; journaled once). |
| Statics `_MtfMath.ClosedIndex`, `MapIndex`, `SymbolRoot`, `IsStale` | The same rules, MC-free, for tests. |

**Journal.** `MTF Data{n} first mapping {t} -> {htf t} (ago i, status s)` (once per session); `MTF forming-bar access on Data{n} (not closedBarsOnly)`.

**VERIFY.** Whether MC stamps HTF bars with their end time ("Probe P2" in the code).

**Tests.** `tests/MtfTests`.

**Snippet** (as in `_MtfProbe`). Declares Data2 as a higher timeframe and reads the high of its last completed bar. `i < 0` means no Data2 bar has closed yet.

```csharp
// StartCalc (HtfStreams must be set before base.StartCalc, which validates the streams)
protected override void StartCalc()
{
	HtfStreams = new[] { 2 };                    // Data2 = the same instrument on a higher timeframe
	base.StartCalc();
}

// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	int i = htfMapped(2);                        // index of the last CLOSED Data2 bar, -1 = none
	if (i < 0) return;
	double h60 = BarsOfData(2).High[i];          // high of the last completed 60m bar
}
```

### 4.13 Rolls (`_RollDetector`, `_RollWindow`)

**Why it exists.** A continuous, unadjusted futures chart (@ES) jumps when it switches from the expiring contract to the next one. That jump is not a market move, but ATRs, gaps, VWAPs and profiles would treat it as one. Roll detection finds the splice bar so that every engine can reset or adjust, and so that your strategy can skip it.

**When to use it.** On every continuous futures chart (almost all §3 examples set it); without it a Warning is traced.

**Enable.** `RollDetection = ERollDetection.Series | Table | SeriesOrTable` (default `Off`) in `Create()`.

**Settings.** `RollGapTicks` (8); `RollDates` (a HashSet of trading days, for `Table`).

**Series or Table.** `Series` reads the splice from the prices and needs no date list, but it is not currently reliable on intraday charts (it over-detects rolls; see App F, Known issues: fix pending in PR #51, so use `Table` with `EventRules.RollDate` meanwhile). On an intraday chart it compares trading-day opens, so it only works when `tradingDayKey` is a Globex (or other evening-rolling) key. `Table` uses fixed dates and is reproducible: `_Test6` (PR #46) fills `RollDates` with `EventRules.RollDate(y, 3/6/9/12)` for 2000–2035, keys days with `_CmeSessionClock.GlobexTradingDayEt` so a splice at the 18:00 ET open falls on the roll day's first bar, and journals `roll(Table n dates from-to digest)` with `_RollDetector.DatesDigest(RollDates)` (FNV-1a 64 of the sorted distinct dates) in the REGGATE header. `EventRules.RollDate` uses the Monday-before-expiry rule from `RollRuleMondayFrom` = 2022-04-01 (ESM22 onward), the Thursday eight days before the third Friday earlier. See §4.3 for a `Table` snippet.

**Series rule.** A trading-day open that gaps ≥ `RollGapTicks` from the prior close on days 1–21 of Mar/Jun/Sep/Dec, or a change of `Bars.Info.Name`.

**API.**

| Member | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| `rollOn(ds)` (virtual) | This bar of stream `ds` is a roll splice. |
| `RollBars` | 1-based Data1 bar numbers of the splices seen so far. |
| `IsRollWindow(tradingDay)` | The day is a roll session or the session just before / after one. False unless rings, FVGs or RollDetection created the roll window. |

**Journal.** Info `VPF-4 roll splice detected tradingDay() bar() name()`; Warning `RollDetection is Off on a futures symbol …`; once per run, a Warning from `_RollDetector.SeriesKeyWarning(series, resolutionType, calendarKeyed)` when `Series` is used on an intraday chart whose `tradingDayKey` is the calendar date (the 18:00 ET splice is never tested); with M-DAILY on, also `DAILY roll offset on a calendar-keyed intraday futures chart …`.

**Tests.** `tests/RollTests`. Event-calendar roll dates: `_EventCalendar.RuleRollDate` / `RollDateFor`.

**Snippet.** Turns on Series detection and skips the splice bar. `nearRoll` is true on the roll session and the sessions on either side, a common "do not trust levels today" flag.

```csharp
// Create
protected override void Create()
{
	base.Create();
	RollDetection = ERollDetection.Series;       // detect splices from the price series
	RollGapTicks = 8;                            // a quarterly-window open gap of >= 8 ticks counts
}

// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();                              // engines that read Data1 have already reset on a roll bar
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	if (rollOn(1)) return;                       // splice bar: its gap is the contract change, not a market move
	bool nearRoll = IsRollWindow(tradingDayKey(Bars.Time[0]));   // roll session +-1
}
```

### 4.14 Bar rings, fair-value gaps and `LevelRef` (`_BarRing`, `_FvgDetector`, `LevelRef`)

These three helpers work together: **rings** keep recent closed bars so helpers can look back over time windows, the **FVG detector** finds one kind of level from those bars, and **`LevelRef`** is the common format in which every level-producing helper hands a level to your strategy.

#### Bar rings — closed-bar history per stream

**Why it exists.** Profile and level helpers need to re-read past windows ("the bars of yesterday's RTH session", "the last 30 minutes before 10:00"). `Bars.High[n]` only reaches back as far as MaxBarsBack and is indexed by bars-ago, not by time. A ring keeps a copy of every closed bar of a stream (time, trading day, OHLCV, up/down volume, RTH flag, roll flag) in a fixed-size buffer, searchable by time.

**When to use it.** Mostly indirectly: DVP (§5.31), bar ladders, range profiles and profile stats request their own rings. Request one yourself when your code needs time-window queries over past bars, or call `SessionBars` on the VP base, as examples 35 and 36 do.

**Enable.** `requireRing(ds, bars, consumer)` in `StartCalc()` **after** `base.StartCalc()` (which clears earlier requests). Capacity is the **sum** of all requests on that stream (`m_ringNeed[ds] = cur + Math.Max(1, bars)`, `_CustomSignal` L1410); `BarRingConfig.capacityBars 0` = automatic, capped at `MaxAutoBars` 40000 (about 29 Globex sessions of 1-minute bars).

**API.**

| Member | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| `Ring(ds)` → `_BarRing` | The ring of stream `ds`, or null when none was requested. |
| `Capacity`, `Count`, `Added`, `Dropped`, `LastBarNumber` | Size, bars held, bars ever added, bars pushed out, last bar number stored. |
| `this[ago]` | Bar by bars-ago (0 = newest). |
| `At(idx)` | Bar by chronological index (0 = oldest held). |
| `IndexAtOrBefore(t)`, `FirstIndexAtOrAfter(t)` | Search by time. |
| `Covers(t0)` | Does the ring still reach back to `t0`? |
| `Window(t0, t1, t0Exclusive)` | The bars in a time window (allocates a list; call once per session, not per bar). |
| `Session(day, rthOnly)` | The bars of one trading day, optionally RTH only. |
| `RingBar` fields `bar`, `time`, `tradingDay`, `o h l c v up down`, `rth`, `rollBar` | One stored bar. |
| `ringBarOf(ds, ago)` | Shortcut for one ring bar. |

**Journal.** `RING Data{n} capacity() consumers()`.

#### Fair-value gaps (FVG)

**Why it exists.** A fair-value gap is a three-bar imbalance: price moved so fast that bar 3's low stays above bar 1's high (bullish) or bar 3's high stays below bar 1's low (bearish). Traders expect price to return to that unfilled zone, and use it as a pullback entry or target. The detector finds such gaps without lookahead, tracks touches and fills, and expires them.

**When to use it.** Pullback entries into a gap, or gaps as targets: example 33.

**Enable.** `Fvgs.enabled = true` in `Create()`. When disabled, `FvgDetector` is null (one Warning on first query).

**Defaults.** `dataStream 1`, `minTicks 1`, `minAtrFrac 0`, `atrLen 14`, `crossSessions false` (all three bars on one trading day, so an opening gap is never an FVG), `rthOnly false`, `maxLive 20`, `maxAgeSessions 5`, `fillOn EFvgFillOn.Close`.

**Rules.** Bullish zone = [bar-1 high, bar-3 low], start edge = bar-1 high; bearish is the mirror. Size must be ≥ max(`minTicks` × tick, `minAtrFrac` × ATR), where ATR is the detector's own Wilder ATR(`atrLen`). A gap is created at bar 3's close and usable from the next bar. A roll invalidates every live gap.

**API.**

| Member | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| `FvgDetector.Live` | Gaps not yet filled, expired or invalidated. |
| `NewThisBar` | A gap completed on this bar, or null. |
| `Atr` | The detector's ATR. |
| `Nearest(price, dir)` | Closest live gap of a direction (distance 0 when price is inside it). |
| `ToLevel(f, startEdge, stream)` | The gap as a `LevelRef`. |
| `Fvg` fields `id`, `dir` (+1 bullish, −1 bearish), `lo`, `hi`, `start`, `createdTime`, `createdDay`, `touched`, `filled`, `expired`, `invalid`, `fillPct`, `ageSessions`, `Size`, `IsLive`, `Tag` (`FVG:U|D:yyyyMMddHHmm`) | One gap: its zone, timing and state. |
| Host `fvgLevel(f, startEdge)` | `ToLevel` plus the `isRollWindow` flag. |

#### `LevelRef` — one format for every level

**Why it exists.** Levels come from many helpers (range profiles, weekly profiles, DVP, profile stats, FVGs, Gate 0). `LevelRef` gives them one shape, so the same strategy or study code can test "price reached a level" regardless of the source, and always knows **from when** the level may be used.

**When to use it.** Whenever you consume levels from more than one helper, or feed them to the level study (§4.17): examples 33 and 42. Producers: `RangeLevels`, `WeekLevels` (VP base), `fvgLevel`, DVP, ProfileStats, `_TestGate0`.

| Field / method | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| `kind` (`ELevelKind`), `price`, `lo`, `hi` | What the level is; a single price, or a zone from `lo` to `hi`. |
| `createdTime` | Usable from this time (no lookahead). |
| `sourceT0`, `sourceT1`, `dataStream` | The time window and stream that produced it. |
| `tag` | Source id, e.g. `RP:…`, `WK:2026W42`, `FVG:U:…`. |
| `quality` | Helper-specific quality score. |
| `crossesRoll`, `isRollWindow` | The source window spans a roll; the level was born around a roll. |
| `Line(...)`, `Zone(...)` | Build a line or zone level. |
| `StudyType`, `StudyKey` | Type and key passed to `_LevelStudy.AddLevel`. |

**Tests.** `tests/VpHelperTests`.

**Snippet.** Enables FVGs (ignoring gaps smaller than 0.25 × ATR), requests a 400-bar ring on Data1, and on each bar reads the previous bar from the ring and turns a new bullish gap into a level. `lv.price` is the gap's start edge (bar-1 high) and is usable from the next bar; levels born around a roll are skipped.

```csharp
// Create
protected override void Create()
{
	base.Create();
	RollDetection = ERollDetection.Series;
	Fvgs.enabled = true;                         // fair-value-gap detector on Data1
	Fvgs.minAtrFrac = 0.25;                      // ignore gaps smaller than 0.25 x ATR(14)
}

// StartCalc (rings are requested after base.StartCalc, which clears earlier requests)
protected override void StartCalc()
{
	base.StartCalc();
	requireRing(1, 400, "my-study");             // keep the last >= 400 closed Data1 bars
}

// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();                              // rings and the detector are filled here
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	_BarRing ring = Ring(1);
	RingBar prev = ring != null && ring.Count > 1 ? ring[1] : default(RingBar);   // the bar before this one
	_FvgDetector det = FvgDetector;
	Fvg gap = det != null ? det.NewThisBar : null;   // a gap completed on this bar, or null
	if (gap == null || gap.dir <= 0) return;
	LevelRef lv = fvgLevel(gap, true);           // start edge (bar-1 high) as a level, usable from the next bar
	if (lv.isRollWindow) return;                 // skip levels born around a roll
}
```

### 4.15 Volume-profile bases (`_VolumeProfileBase`, `_VolumeProfileFuturesBase`)

**Why it exists.** Volume-profile trading reads where volume traded, not just where price went: the point of control (POC, the busiest price), the value area (VA, about 70 % of volume), high- and low-volume nodes (HVN/LVN), and POCs that price has not revisited ("naked"). The VP bases build these profiles from MC data, add up to three VWAP slots, combine levels into confluence zones and targets, and provide the Phase 3 helpers (range, weekly and bar-ladder profiles, profile stats). The futures base adds the CME clock, IB/RTH stats, VPF-5 order flow and the CME holiday hooks.

**When to use it.** Any strategy that needs profile levels or order flow. 31 of the 42 §3 examples run on `_VolumeProfileFuturesBase`: 28 derive from it directly (3, 6–8, 10, 11, 14, 15, 17, 19, 20, 22, 25–34, 36–39, 41, 42) and examples 21, 35 and 40 inherit it through another example. The other 11 run on `_CustomSignal`: 10 (1, 2, 4, 5, 9, 12, 13, 16, 18, 23) derive from it directly and example 24 derives from example 18 (`_Ex18FirstHourBreak`); move one onto the VP base before calling anything below.

**Enable.** Derive from `_VolumeProfileFuturesBase`. Unlike `_CustomSignal`, these bases expose `[Input]`s:
- `HvnPercentile .8`, `LvnPercentile .2`, `MinNodeTicks 3`, `TrackNakedPoc true`, `NakedPocTickTolerance 1`;
- `Vwap1..3 Type/Length/FromDate/DataStream` (Vwap1 = Session on DS1; 2 and 3 Disabled);
- `DevelopingSource`, `DevProfileSourceMode`, `DevProfileDataStream`, `ValueAreaMethod`, `LookaheadAudit false`.

**Main API.**

| Group | Members | Plain meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Profiles | `ExtraVolumeProfileForBar([ds,] barIndex)` (1-based), `RecentCompletedProfiles(ds, count, asOfBar)`, `NakedPocs(ds[, asOf])`, `IsNewVolumeProfile(...)` | The profile a bar belongs to; the last finished profiles; POCs not yet revisited; a new profile started on this bar. |
| Confluence | `GetConfluenceZones`, `GetTopConfluenceZones([n])`, `GetConfluenceTargets(price, EVpDirection[, n])`, `GetConfluenceTargetZones` | Price zones where several levels (POC, VA edges, VWAPs, naked POCs) cluster; the strongest ones; the next zones in a trade's direction, for targets. |
| VWAP slots | `CurrentVwap1..3`, `CurrentVwap1Sigma`, `Vwap1Active` | The configured VWAPs, slot 1's standard deviation, whether slot 1 has a value. |
| Auction and value | `ValueRelationship`, `IsPoorHigh`/`IsPoorLow`, `HasUnfinishedAuction`, `SinglePrints`, `ProfileShape`, `ValueMigrationScore`/`ValueMigrationState`, `Composite(stream, n)`, `IsBalanced`, `BalanceRange` | Where today's value sits vs yesterday's; extremes without a clean auction (likely revisited); thin single-print areas; profile shape; whether value is moving; an n-session composite; whether the market is balancing and its range. |
| Futures profiles | `RthProfile()`, `GlobexProfile()`, `PriorRthProfile()` | Today's RTH and overnight profiles; yesterday's RTH profile. |
| Range profiles | `RangeProfile(stream, t0, t1)`, `RangeProfileAfter`, `RangeProfileBars`, `OpenRange`/`CloseRange`/`RangeHandleProfile`, `RangeStatsOf`, `RangeLevels` | A profile over any time window (e.g. the opening hour), its stats, and its levels as `LevelRef`s. |
| Weekly | `Week(weeksAgo)`, `WeeklyComposite(n)`, `CompletedWeeks`, `WeekKey`, `WeekLevels` | Weekly profiles and their levels. |
| Ladders | `Ladder(ago[, minQuality])`, `LadderAt(time)`, `HtfLadder(ds, ago)`, `LadderProfile(b)` | A per-bar price ladder (volume at each price inside one bar). |
| Other | `ProfileEntropy(e, mmN)`, `SessionBars(day, rthOnly)` | How spread-out a profile is; the ring bars of one session (§4.14). |

**Order flow (futures base).** Why: bid/ask volume shows who was aggressive inside a bar. Example 38 sets `OrderFlowEnabled = true` itself; example 34 only enables `BarLadders`, which switches `OrderFlowEnabled` on (`_VolumeProfileFuturesBase` L133, VP-3). Enable with `OrderFlowEnabled = true`; configure `FlowCfg` (`_OrderFlow.FlowConfig`) and `FlowTickSource`. **BarUpDown needs `TradeVolume`:** `FlowCfg.BuildVolumeOn` defaults to `VolumeBasis.Unknown`, and `BarUpDownQualifies` only accepts `VolumeBasis.TradeVolume`, so without the explicit setting the BarUpDown tier reports `Unavailable`. This is a deliberate fail-safe (MultiCharts cannot tell trade-volume bars from tick-count bars); set `FlowCfg.BuildVolumeOn = VolumeBasis.TradeVolume` after checking the chart's volume setting.

| Member | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| `Flow` | The order-flow engine. |
| `Footprint(barsAgo)` | Volume at each price split into bid and ask for one bar. |
| `BarDelta(barsAgo)`, `CumDelta(barsAgo)` | Ask minus bid volume for the bar; running total since the reset key (session or RTH). |
| `DiagonalImbalances(barsAgo, ratio = 3.0, minVol = 10)`, `StackedImbalances(barsAgo, ratio = 3.0, minVol = 10, minStack = 3)` | Prices where one side dominates the diagonal neighbour; several such prices in a row. |
| `Absorption(barsAgo, side, edgeTicks = 2, volMult = 2.0, holdTicks = 1)` | Heavy aggression at the bar's edge that did not move price. |

**Phase 3 configs.** `RangeProfiles`, `WeeklyProfiles`, `ProfileStats`, `BarLadders` (`enabled false` by default; defaults in the `configs` of `_RangeProfile`/`_WeeklyProfiles`/`_ProfileStats`/`_BarLadder`).

**Journal.** `VPB-1 own profile: source() mode() approximate() valueArea() audit()`; `RP build {tag} Data{n} [t0..t1] bars(a/b) poc() va() approx() roll()`; `RP coverage …`; `LADDER HTF Data{n} {t} coverage() -> totals-only`.

**Tests.** `tests/ProfileTests`, `tests/VpHelperTests`, `tests/OrderFlowTests`.

### 4.16 M-PORT publisher and `_PortfolioRiskManager` (`_PortMM`)

**Why it exists.** In Portfolio Trader several strategies trade at once, and their risks add up: three long equity-index trades are one big bet. M-PORT has each strategy publish its state (`pm.*` keys in `MyPortfolioData`); a separate money-management signal reads all of them and sizes and limits risk across the book (risk parity, heat and cluster caps, drawdown steps, daily loss stops).

**When to use it.** Only in Portfolio Trader with more than one strategy: examples 37 and 40. On a single chart the publisher is inactive.

**Enable.**
- The publisher is automatic in PT (`PortfolioPublish`). Override as needed: `PortfolioFamily`, `PortfolioScore`, `PortfolioHalted`, `PortfolioCluster` (default from the symbol root: ES→EQIDX…), `PortfolioRegime`.
- Apply `_PortfolioRiskManager` as the portfolio's Money Management signal. It has no `[Input]`s; subclass it and override `Config` or `ConfigPath` (JSON).

**Keys (`_PortKeys`).**

| Group | Keys | Plain meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Identity | `pm.ver`, `pm.id`, `pm.family`, `pm.cluster` | Who is publishing, and which correlated group it belongs to. |
| Equity and returns | `pm.eq`, `pm.pnl`, `pm.ret`, `pm.uRet`, `pm.vol` | Equity, P&L, realised and unrealised return, volatility. |
| Risk | `pm.R$`, `pm.qtyCode`, `pm.riskNew`, `pm.riskOpen`, `pm.openR` | Dollar risk per R, requested size, new and open risk. |
| State | `pm.state`, `pm.side`, `pm.regime`, `pm.regimeP`, `pm.score`, `pm.stamp`, `pm.day`, `pm.pos` | `EPortState` `{Flat, Armed, Long, Short, Exiting, Halted}`, direction, regime, score, freshness, position. |
| Events | `pm.evt*` | Event flags. |
| Written by the MM | `pm.mult`, `pm.heat`, `pm.dd`, `pm.rho` | Size multiplier set by the MM; book heat; drawdown; correlation. |

**`PortMmConfig` defaults.**
- Sizing: `sizing RiskParity`, `bookVolTarget 0.10`, `riskPerTrade 0.005` (library default; the house rule is 1% risk per trade).
- Risk caps: `heatCap 0.04`, `clusterCap 0.02`, `rhoMerge 0.9`, `rhoWin 60`, `rhoMinDays 40`, `maxPositions 6`.
- Drawdown: `ddSteps {5%→0.5, 10%→0.25, 15%→0}`, `ddRecoverHighDays 20`.
- Loss stops and kills: `dailyLossStop 0.02`, `dailyLossFlatten 0.03`, `stratKillR -8`, `stratKillTrades 20`.
- Other: `staleBars 3`, `minContracts 1`, `idmCap 2.5`.

**API in your strategy.** `portfolioArm(int side, double entry, double stop, int qtyCode)` announces a planned entry (called for you by `sizeForRisk` for Data1); `portfolioStop(double)` only sets the stop used for `pm.riskOpen` while the position is open; it publishes nothing by itself (`pm.R$` and `pm.riskNew` keep the armed values).

**Journal.** Info `PORT publish on: id() family() cluster()`; Journal `PORT book multiplier x at t`. MM lines go to Output (`PORT Warning/Info`).

**Tests.** `tests/PortMMTests`. Live checks use `_TestPortArm` + `_TestPortMM`. Worked snippet: §5.23.

### 4.17 A2-33 level study (`_LevelStudy`) and Gate 0 (`_Gate0`)

**Why it exists.** Before trading a level type ("prior POC", "naked VWAP"), you want evidence that price reacts there more than at a random price. The level study compares each registered level with matched control prices (random prices at a similar distance) and measures reaction and survival ("death" = price trades through by `deathR`). Gate 0 is the research harness that runs this for whole level families against seeded placebos.

**When to use it.** Research, not live trading: example 41, and the Gate 0 runs in §2.

**Level study.**
- Enable with `LevelStudyConfig.enabled = true`.
- Defaults: `controls 20`, `distLo .9`, `distHi 1.1`, `reactionBars 60`, `sigmaBars 60`, `touchTicks 0`, `maxAge 15`, `deathR 1`, `seed 33`, `from`/`to`, `csvPath ""`, `controlsEitherSide false`, `reactionAfterTouch false`.
- Register prior-session levels on the session's first bar with `levelStudyAdd(type, price, ageSessions, quality = NaN, key = null)`.
- Results: Info `A2-33 RESULT events() types() window()` plus a summary per type at RUN-END, and an optional CSV.
- No strategy on `main` enables `LevelStudyConfig`: `_Gate0` drives `_LevelStudy` directly. The catalogue and the `LevelStudyConfig` comment in `_CustomSignal` name `_Test1`, but `_Test1` is a plain `SignalObject` (discrepancy, Appendix F).

**Gate 0** (`Gate0Engine`, `EGate0Mode/Family/Canary`, `Gate0Bar/Intent/Profile/Vwap/Atr/Session`): MC-free, hosted by `_TestGate0`; use in **§2**, snippet in §5.29.

**Tests.** `tests/LevelStudyTests`, `tests/Gate0Tests`. Snippet: §5.28.

### 4.18 Utility engines

**Why they exist.** Shared maths so that strategies do not reimplement statistics. All are MC-free and deterministic.

**When to use them.** Profile-shape and bimodality filters (worked snippet §5.25): example 35 uses `_ProfileStats.KdeSweep`, `BootstrapPoc` and `BootstrapSeed`; example 36 uses `_ProfileStats.Histogram`, `ProfileMixture` and `MixtureSeed` (the mixture fit runs on `_Gmm` internally; neither example calls `_Gmm` directly); `_Rng` wherever a reproducible random choice is needed (used by Gate 0, the regime engine, AVWAP and profile stats).

| Engine | Members | Plain meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `_Gmm` | `Fit`, `Fit1D`, `FitK`, `FitOnce`, `LogDensity`, `Density1D`, `Order`, `ParamCount`; `GmmModel`, `GmmFitConfig` | Gaussian-mixture fits: does a session's volume form one hump or two (bimodal = two value areas)? |
| `_Rng` | `Mix`, `Seed(params long[])`, `Hash(string)` | Deterministic random numbers: the same inputs give the same draws on every run. |
| `_ProfileStats` | `Entropy`, `MmN`, `KL`, `KLKde*`, `KdeOnGrid` | How spread-out a profile is; how different two profiles are; smoothed profile. |
| | `KdeSweep`, `BootstrapPoc`, `BootstrapSeed` | KDE bandwidth sweep with a Stable/Flat/Bimodal verdict; bootstrap POC stability (example 35). |
| | `Histogram`, `ProfileMixture`, `MixtureSeed` | Profile histogram from ring bars; Gaussian-mixture fit of the profile (example 36). |
| | `ParkinsonPerBar`, `ParkinsonSessionPrice`, `EwmaForecast`, `WilderAtr` | Range-based volatility estimates and forecasts; ATR. |
| | `AggregateBars`; `GmmSession.FromBars` | Combine bars; build a GMM input from session bars. |
| `_SignalJournal` | `PROBE_ID`, `Describe()` | A CS-0 probe description only; no runtime role. |

---

## 5 — Feature reference (catalogue §2.4 base-feature map)

One subsection per row of the catalogue's §2.4 table (31 rows), in the catalogue's order. **Status** is what `origin/main` @ `a07257a` contains, checked against the code (where it differs from the catalogue's Status column, that is said). **Signatures** are the key members, pulled by script from the tree (the full list is in Appendix E). **Snippets** are minimal strategy fragments; each was compiled as a small class (`_SnNN`) with the same checker as the examples (0 errors). The comment on each method says where the code lives: `Create()` for configuration, `StartCalc()` for per-run state, `CalcBar()` for per-bar logic. ### 5.1 F-DEV — Own developing profile, VA methods, lookahead audit (VPB-1/2/3/4)

**Status on main a07257a.** ✓ Done on main (MC verification of the truncation audit still owed).

**Why it exists.** MC's built-in developing profile cannot be restarted at the RTH open or audited for lookahead; the own profile gives a per-session POC/VA you can trust intraday.

**Where.** `_VolumeProfileBase.Strategy.CS` · `_VolumeProfileBase` (`DevelopingSource`, `DevelopingExtra`); `_VolumeProfileFuturesBase.Strategy.CS` (`OwnProfileWindow`, `RthProfile`)

**Key signatures.**

```csharp
public enum EDevelopingSource { MultiCharts = 0, Own = 1 }   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:744
[Input] public EDevelopingSource DevelopingSource { get; set; }   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:756
public VpExtra DevelopingExtra(int stream)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:936
public enum EOwnProfileWindow { MultiChartsProfile = 0, GlobexDay = 1, Rth = 2 }   // _VolumeProfileFuturesBase · _VolumeProfileFuturesBase:467
protected EOwnProfileWindow OwnProfileWindow { get; set; }   // _VolumeProfileFuturesBase · _VolumeProfileFuturesBase:473
public VpExtra RthProfile()   // _VolumeProfileFuturesBase · _VolumeProfileFuturesBase:524
```

**Snippet** (compiled as `_Sn01`, base `_VolumeProfileFuturesBase`).

Switches the VP base to its own RTH-anchored profile and journals the developing POC and value area each bar. `dev` is null before the first RTH bar.

```csharp
// Create
protected override void Create()
{
	base.Create();
	DevelopingSource = EDevelopingSource.Own;      // VP-base [Input]: set in code, never exposed
	OwnProfileWindow = EOwnProfileWindow.Rth;      // the developing profile restarts at 09:30 ET
}

// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();                                // the profile is updated inside base.CalcBar()
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	VpExtra dev = RthProfile();                    // developing RTH profile (null before the first RTH bar)
	if (dev != null && journalOn())
		trace(ETraceLevel.Journal, $"dev POC {dev.poc} VA {dev.val}-{dev.vah}");
}
```

**Used in examples.** example 21 (§3.17), example 22 (§3.25), example 26 (§3.26), example 27 (§3.27), example 29 (§3.28), example 30 (§3.29), example 31 (§3.31), example 28 (§3.35), example 32 (§3.36), example 34 (§3.38); inherited by example 35.

**Pitfalls.**

- `DevelopingSource` and `OwnProfileWindow` are VP-base properties; `DevelopingSource` is an `[Input]` on the base. Set both in `Create()`; do not add your own `[Input]`.
- `RthProfile()` and `PriorRthProfile()` return null unless `DevelopingSource = Own` and `OwnProfileWindow = Rth`.
- Read the profile only after `base.CalcBar()` and only on a closed bar; the developing profile includes the current bar.

### 5.2 F-PROF — Completed-profile analytics (VPB-6)

**Status on main a07257a.** ✓ Done on main.

**Why it exists.** Yesterday's profile tells you where the market accepted value; poor highs, single prints and value migration flag auctions likely to be revisited or extended.

**Where.** `_VolumeProfileBase.Strategy.CS` · `_VolumeProfileBase` (`ValueRelationship`, `IsPoorHigh/Low`, `SinglePrints`, `ProfileShape`, `IsNakedVah/Val`, `AcceptanceOutsideValue`, `ValueMigrationScore`, `RecentCompletedProfiles`); `_VolumeProfileFuturesBase.PriorRthProfile`

**Key signatures.**

```csharp
public ProfileMath.ValueRel ValueRelationship(VpExtra prev, VpExtra cur)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1081
public bool IsPoorHigh(VpExtra e, double fraction = 0.5, int minRows = 2)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1088
public bool IsPoorLow(VpExtra e, double fraction = 0.5, int minRows = 2)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1095
public List<double[]> SinglePrints(VpExtra e, int minTicks, double pct = 0.2)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1115
public ProfileMath.Shape ProfileShape(VpExtra e)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1127
public bool IsNakedVah(VpExtra e, int asOfBar)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1157
public bool IsNakedVal(VpExtra e, int asOfBar)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1158
public int AcceptanceOutsideValue(VpExtra reference, bool above, int minCloses)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1164
public int? ValueMigrationScore(int stream, int n = 3, double minChangeAtrFraction = 0.10, int atrLen = 20, bool includeDeveloping = true, double dailyAtr = 0)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1198
public int ValueMigrationScore(int stream, double atr20Daily, int n = 3, double minChangeFraction = 0.10, bool includeDeveloping = true)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1309
public List<VpExtra> RecentCompletedProfiles(int datastream, int count, int asOfBar)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1495
public VpExtra PriorRthProfile()   // _VolumeProfileFuturesBase · _VolumeProfileFuturesBase:548
public static List<int[]> SinglePrints(double[] vols, int vaLo, int vaHi, double pct, int minRows)   // _ProfileMath · _ProfileMath:144
public static Shape ProfileShape(double[] vols, double vaPercent)   // _ProfileMath · _ProfileMath:164
public static bool ValueMigrationScore(IList<double> completedPocs, int n, double developingPoc, double minChange, out int score)   // _ProfileMath · _ProfileMath:247
public static List<double[]> TpoSinglePrints(IDictionary<long, int> tpo, int minRows, double tick)   // Gate0Profile · _Gate0:140
```

**Snippet** (compiled as `_Sn02`, base `_VolumeProfileFuturesBase`).

Reads yesterday's completed RTH profile: is its high poor, how many closes are above its VAH, and how value has migrated over 3 sessions. `v` is null until enough sessions exist; ≥ +2 = value migrating up, ≤ −2 = down.

```csharp
// Create
protected override void Create()
{
	base.Create();
	DevelopingSource = EDevelopingSource.Own;      // build our own profile ...
	OwnProfileWindow = EOwnProfileWindow.Rth;      // ... per RTH session, so PriorRthProfile() exists
}

// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	VpExtra prior = PriorRthProfile();             // last COMPLETED RTH profile
	if (prior == null) return;
	bool poorHigh = IsPoorHigh(prior);             // fraction 0.5, minRows 2 (defaults)
	int closesAbove = AcceptanceOutsideValue(prior, true, 2);
	int? v = ValueMigrationScore(1, 3);            // null until 4 completed sessions (20 without a daily ATR)
	if (journalOn())
		trace(ETraceLevel.Journal, $"poorHigh {poorHigh} closesAboveVAH {closesAbove} V {v}");
}
```

**Used in examples.** example 11 (§3.14), example 21 (§3.17), example 6 (§3.23), example 22 (§3.25), example 26 (§3.26), example 27 (§3.27), example 30 (§3.29), example 28 (§3.35), example 32 (§3.36), example 41 (§3.41); inherited by example 35.

**Pitfalls.**

- `ValueMigrationScore` has two overloads: `(stream, n, ...)` returns `int?` (null when history is short); `(stream, atr20Daily, n, ...)` returns `int`.
- Analytics take a completed `VpExtra`; passing the developing profile gives values that change during the session.

### 5.3 F-COMP — Composites of N sessions, IsBalanced / BalanceRange (VPB-7)

**Status on main a07257a.** ✓ Done on main.

**Why it exists.** One session is noisy; a composite of several sessions shows the value area the market has agreed on over days, and whether it is balancing.

**Where.** `_VolumeProfileBase.Strategy.CS` · `_VolumeProfileBase.Composite`, `IsBalanced`, `BalanceRange`

**Key signatures.**

```csharp
public VpExtra Composite(int stream, int n)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1249
public bool IsBalanced(int stream, int n, double minVaOverlap = 0.6)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1275
public bool BalanceRange(int stream, int n, bool union, out double high, out double low)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1285
public static bool IsBalanced(IList<double> vahs, IList<double> vals, double minOverlap)   // _ProfileMath · _ProfileMath:226
```

**Snippet** (compiled as `_Sn03`, base `_VolumeProfileFuturesBase`).

Builds a 3-session composite and checks whether the sessions overlap (≥ 60 %), i.e. the market is balancing; `BalanceRange` returns the balance high and low.

```csharp
// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	VpExtra comp = Composite(1, 3);                // last 3 completed sessions on Data1
	if (comp == null) return;
	bool balanced = IsBalanced(1, 3, 0.6);         // value areas overlap >= 60 %
	double hi, lo;
	if (BalanceRange(1, 3, true, out hi, out lo) && journalOn())
		trace(ETraceLevel.Journal, $"comp VA {comp.val}-{comp.vah} balanced {balanced} range {lo}-{hi}");
}
```

**Used in examples.** example 21 (§3.17), example 26 (§3.26), example 27 (§3.27), example 29 (§3.28), example 31 (§3.31), example 28 (§3.35), example 34 (§3.38).

**Pitfalls.**

- A composite has `crossesRoll`; on unadjusted continuous data, a composite across a roll mixes two contracts. Check it or set `RollDetection`.
- Compute the composite once per session (first bar), not on every bar; it is rebuilt on each call.

### 5.4 F-NPOC — Naked-POC tracking

**Status on main a07257a.** ✓ Done on main.

**Why it exists.** A POC that price has not traded back to is an unfinished auction that price often returns to, a common target or reaction level.

**Where.** `_VolumeProfileBase.Strategy.CS` · `TrackNakedPoc`, `NakedPocTickTolerance` ([Input]s), `NakedPocs(ds[, asOfBar])`, `VpExtra.IsNakedPoc`

**Key signatures.**

```csharp
public bool IsNakedPoc(int asOfBar)   // _VolumeProfileBase.VpExtra · _VolumeProfileBase:181
[Input] public bool TrackNakedPoc { get; set; }   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:501
public List<VpExtra> NakedPocs(int datastream)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1513
public List<VpExtra> NakedPocs(int datastream, int asOfBar)   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:1524
```

**Snippet** (compiled as `_Sn04`, base `_VolumeProfileFuturesBase`).

Walks the naked POCs and logs when price is within 2 ticks of one, a revisit of an unfinished auction.

```csharp
// Create
protected override void Create()
{
	base.Create();
	TrackNakedPoc = true;                          // VP-base [Input]: set in code
}

// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	foreach (VpExtra p in NakedPocs(1))            // newest unfinished auction first
	{
		bool atPoc = Math.Abs(Bars.Close[0] - p.poc) <= 2 * tickSize();   // within 2 ticks
		if (atPoc && journalOn())
			trace(ETraceLevel.Journal, $"at naked POC {p.poc}");
	}
}
```

**Used in examples.** example 6 (§3.23), example 41 (§3.41).

**Pitfalls.**

- `NakedPocs` is empty unless `TrackNakedPoc = true`.
- Age the levels yourself (e.g. by index in `RecentCompletedProfiles`) if a rule caps age in sessions.

### 5.5 F-HVN — KDE HVN/LVN nodes on a 1-tick grid

**Status on main a07257a.** ✓ Done on main.

**Why it exists.** High-volume nodes act as magnets and low-volume nodes as fast-move zones; price inside an LVN tends to travel through it.

**Where.** `_VolumeProfileBase.Strategy.CS` · `VpExtra.hvns` / `VpExtra.lvns` (`List<Node>`: low, high, peak, peakVol, strength, isPoc); `MaxLvnFractionOfRange`

**Key signatures.**

```csharp
public double MaxLvnFractionOfRange { get; set; }   // _VolumeProfileBase · _VolumeProfileBase:647
```

**Snippet** (compiled as `_Sn05`, base `_VolumeProfileFuturesBase`).

Takes the LVNs of a 20-session composite and logs when the close sits inside one; inside an LVN, a fast move through is more likely.

```csharp
// CalcBar
protected override void CalcBar()
{
	base.CalcBar();
	if (Bars.Status != EBarState.Close) return;
	VpExtra comp = Composite(1, 20);
	if (comp == null || comp.lvns == null) return;
	foreach (Node n in comp.lvns)                  // low-volume nodes: [low, high], peak = node centre
	{
		bool inside = Bars.Close[0] >= n.low && Bars.Close[0] <= n.high;
		if (inside && journalOn())
			trace(ETraceLevel.Journal, $"inside LVN {n.low}-{n.high}");
	}
}
```

**Used in examples.** example 29 (§3.28), example 33 (§3.37), example 34 (§3.38).

**Pitfalls.**

- Nodes come from a smoothed (KDE) profile; their edges are not exact price prints.
- HVN/LVN lists can be empty on thin profiles; check for null and Count.

}
